So adjacent sidechain distances seem okay. Maybe severe clash is between backbone atoms of nonadjacent residues, e.g., carbonyl oxygen of one residue and amide nitrogen of another forming a hydrogen bond that is too short. Let's inspect backbone-backbone distances across secondary structure. Could be O...N distances <2.0. We can search for backbone atoms with close coordinates.

Maybe easier: look for pairs of atoms with same or nearby coordinates. Let's scan for atoms with x,y,z within ~1.5. We can use a grid approach mentally. Let's sort by x coordinate and look for clusters with close y,z. Since severe clash likely <2, x difference <2. We can examine atoms in x bins of 2 Å. But many. Yet we can scan for exact close coordinates. Let's create bins by x and list atoms with y,z. Maybe find pairs with y and z also close.

Alternatively, we can compute all pairwise distances between atoms of residues that are within 5 Å in sequence? But severe clash may be between residues far apart. Could use contact map by residue centers. Let's compute residue center coordinates and find close residue pairs. Then inspect atoms.

Maybe the structure is a single chain with a known fold; severe clash might be between two residues that are far apart but sidechains point into each other. We can identify by looking at residue centers. Let's compute approximate centers for each residue and find pairs with center distance <4 Å. Then inspect atom distances. Since severe clash likely between sidechain atoms, residue centers may be close.

This exhaustive scan of adjacent bins only found peptide bonds and H-bonds. But severe clash could be between atoms in non-adjacent x bins if x difference >2? No, if distance <2, x difference must be <2. So our scan of adjacent bins should catch any pair with distance <2. We compared each bin to next bin, but not all pairs within same bin? We did some but not exhaustively for same bin. However same bin atoms are often within same residue or adjacent residues, and we checked many. But there could be non-adjacent residues with x within same bin and y,z close. We need check within each bin more thoroughly, not just adjacent bins. Let's inspect each bin for atoms from different residues that are close. Since x difference <2, we need y,z difference <2. We can look for atoms in same bin with similar y,z. Let's do that.

This is too much. But maybe the severe clash is between residues that are far apart in sequence but close in 3D, and we haven't found because we only looked at residue centers and some pairs. Let's think of the fold. The chain seems to have two lobes: N-terminal residues -2 to 20 in one region, residues 21-40 in another, residues 41-60 in another, residues 61-85 in another. There may be contacts between lobe 1 and lobe 4, etc. We need find any pair of atoms with distance <2. Could be between residues from different lobes that are close in 3D but far in sequence. Let's identify spatial proximity between different segments.

Segment A: residues -2 to 20, coordinates roughly x 4-31, y 0-27, z -5 to10.
Segment B: residues 21 to 40, coordinates roughly x -12 to11, y -26 to0, z -7 to3.
Segment C: residues 41 to 60, coordinates roughly x -22 to-10, y -26 to-5, z -5 to12.
Segment D: residues 61 to 85, coordinates roughly x -10 to13, y -8 to25, z -13 to6.

Potential inter-segment contacts:
- A and D: A has x up to 31, D up to 13. Some overlap in x 4-13, y 0-25, z -5 to6. Could be close. For example residue 4 TYR (x20-25,y18-21,z3-9) and residue 84 TYR (x1-3,y20-24,z-11 to-3) far in x. Residue 1 VAL (x19-21,y23-27,z3-5) and residue 82 ASP (x7-10,y22-25,z-4 to-8) distance maybe 10. Residue 0 SER (x23-26,y22-25,z1-4) and residue 82 ASP far. Residue 2 SER (x21-22,y21-24,z-2 to0) and residue 83 ASN (x5-7,y17-19,z-9 to-11) far. Residue 3 ASP (x22-27,y18-21,z0-2) and residue 81 HIS (x9-13,y16-18,z-4 to-5) distance ~10. Residue 4 TYR and residue 80 ALA (x6-9,y19-21,z-3 to0) maybe distance ~12. Residue 5 THR (x16-18,y18-22,z0-3) and residue 79 GLU (x0-6,y19-20,z-5 to-3) distance ~10.
